RUDDICK'S DAY OUT.

--- Ruddick was in his element against Batley, and it is a moot point whether it would not be d always manage the scrums. tie vs a workedbl e tot hleetmhirearm tieu lady well on Saturday. Nicholas was always in his place, and from his advisable work on Saturday, I am inclined to the opinion that he is of much greater value away from the scrum than in charge of It. It was he chiefly who opened ,111) for the threequarter bouts of passing. Jolley was happy, but he did not come so often into the picture as he did at Huddersfield. The reason for this was because he was called upon less frequently. At punting he had no equal on the field, and he cleveily intercepted the ball when Batley' thought they were goihg all the way. His dodging led to the too eager Batley- ites often coming to grief. , Weight cannot beat Jolley, if he can get a, char,ce to dodge.

WARRINGTON "A" WIN AT' HOME. Baker Again Gives a Fine Show.

it was satisfactory to see such aline gate"" at Wilderspool. About 3,000 spectators turned out to see the "A" team defeat Leigh Sham- rocks by 11 points to three. The start was very uninteresting, and the spectators were get- Ling " somewhat- irritated. However; when Leigh.. were awarded a penalty there was some. enthusiasm, and the Warrington supporters heaved a 'sigh of relief when the ball struck the Upright: • . -

After 20 minutes' play the Warrington pack brightened-up,- and in midfield a passing move- ment was initiated by Dainteth, who sent out to Baker. He smartly picked up and returned to Dalnteth, who threw the ball to Davies. That player made a determined effort to get over, but was tackled before he became really dan- gerous.

Leigh forced matters, and Stirrup, intercept- ing a pass, struggled for the line. Brown came to the rescue just in time, and once again the spectators -breathed evenly. Waywell was the first to score. Cleverly evading the halves, he made a bee line for the corner, where he scored a try, which Critchley, however, failed to con- vert.

Leigh's try was a very lucky one. Mole dribbled down the left wing and just as he got over the line he was jostled by three of the Warripgton men. • The ball was knocked down In the struggle, and the referee allowed Me points. The goal kick was missed. Just before the interval, Critchley tried for goal, but the ball struck the upright. King was sent off by the referee.

WAYAVELL SPRINGS A SURPRISE.

Warrington were by far the better team in the second half, and early on. Baker and Waywell .made smart runs. A surprise score came in a few minutes. Waywell was running across and took a running kick at goal. The ball sailed right between the sticks, to the sound of tumultuous cheering. The Warrington men bustled up and set about their task in a deter-, mined manner, and when Brown, .the plucky half, forced his way through a scrimmage and got over, everyone was ,in a good humour. Critchley 'again struek the Post with the ensu- ing kick. Baker added another three points just before the call- of time. He had a ,struggle for it, for he started about midfield, but in his clever manner he went over. Critchley again failed goal. -noWn the line Naylor dashed, but Baker just missed the ,pass. Some thought .this a faulty nicA-e, but; there very little' cane fOr a Try, as the Leigh men were forming well down, and would have resisted any dual effort.

It was a good win, and no doubt we shall see good "gates" ril all the "A" team matches if they maintain their present form.

THE MATCH OF TO-DAY.

Broughton Rangers' Visit.

The visit of Broughton Rangers is always a popular event at Wilderspool, and I anticipate seeing a large crowd at the Warrington enclo- sure to-day, when the old rivals meet. The teams in the past have put up some good fights, and a repetition of past doings may be expected? Last year the corresponding match resulted ii a bloodless draw, but at Broughton the Rangers found Warrington out of forth and inflicted a defeat of eight points to nil upon the "Wire- pullers." This season Broughton Bangers have so far been going very groggy, and on Saturday they met with a severe trouncing upon their new pitch at Broughton Cliff at the hands of Swin- ton. This quite unexpected defeat has caused the Rangers some anxiety about the -team they should send to -encounter such a'doughty lot as Warrington prove at home, and there was some re-shuffling of the men ere they decided on the team.

If Warrington play like they did against Leigh and Batley I cannot see why they should not win. Given the same form, the local lads ought to emerge victors by at least eight points. 'Certainly the Rangers will have a stiff thing on hand to do as they did last year and make a draw. The referee will be Mr. H. Robinson, of Bradford. The duties of touch-judges will be performed by Messrs. E. Bennett, Liverpool, and. R. Boydell, Leigh. The Warrington team has undergone no change. The committee were so satisfied with their wOrk at Batley 'that they are continuing

SOME OF THE GAMES. Great Sankey v. Weston.

This match was played at Weston,- before a good ciowd of spectators. The 'visitors won The toss, and klUlse kicked off. The home team ,worked their way ,to the visitors' quarters, where Ditchfield cleared his lines and passed otit to Darlington. lie ran the ball towards the home goal, where Bennett made a poor attempt to score for the visitors. The home defence were kept very busy, and Nancollis saved some good shots, until Illidge beat him at the end of 15 minutes' play. WeSton made a good attempt on the visitors' goal, but could not break down the defence. Bennett scored a second goal,for the visitors, and they led at half-time by two clear goals. . On resuming, Sankey made tracks for goal, but Darlington got into an ' offside position, which gave relief to the home defence. Sankey were having the best of the game, and Nancollis was a host in himself. The visitors were awarded a penalty for rough play, from which Ditchfield scored. The home forwards were unable to do much, owing to the poor support they got from their half-backs, and Smith broke away on his own and scored their only point. Stephens scored, a fourth goal for the visitors and the game ended: Great Sankey,. four goals; Weston, one goal.

Orford St. John's v. Stockton Heath "A."

The match between these clubs took place at Long-lane, Orford, when the following players lined out :---40r1ord, St. John's-,-Forbes; Smith and Millward ; Dennie, Atherton and Matthews; .1. Smith, Tickle, Meachin, Hankin- son and T. Sudell. Stockton Heath "A "—H. Holmes; P. Boyd and Houghton; Astall, W. L. Spencer and Whitfield; S. Lloyd, J. Vernon, T. Smith, Burdon and Percival. Referee W. Dennett. The home team won the toss and the visitors kicked off.' .Their right wing combined well and made for the home line, of defence ;k Millward eleiiring in- good -Style, Whilst Spencer -did some fine work in mid-field for the visitors. Smith and Tickle on the right„wing made a .grand run, beating their opponents easily, but .the former placed the ball in_touch when in a good .position; „Percival, for the visitors, sent ,wide of the goal With a good slug, while Vernon and Lloyd was well lookeds_aiter by Atherton. A free-kick to the home , team relieved their defenee, and Sudell raced away on the left and centred finely, causing lielmes to handle for the first time. At this period the home team were playing finely. The visitors' backs made some Weak returns, resulting in •Tickle scoring with a good shot. This reverse 'roused. the visitors, and they made a momentary raid on .the home goal, where Pereival missed a chance of equalising by kicking wide. From the goal- kick HankinSon got possession and made a good attempt by pitting the crossbar. Holmes Cleared with several players round him, while shortly :after Tickle scored his second goal. The home team were having the best of the game. The visitors made .an appeal for "hands," stopped playing, and Meachin went on and , scored. The same player added a fourth goal just before half-time,-when the score was four goals to nil in favour of the home team. On resuming, the Heathens"" were the first to attack and they obtained a corner, but could not -improve upon it. The "Lads" next. had a turn, and Holmes was soon busy, making some good saves, but eventually HankinsOn beat him with a beauty. Offside against T. Smith, the visitors' centre, when favourably placed, threw a chance away, as also did Vernon by dallying in front of goal. The Visitors were now show- ing signs of distress and they could not hold the home forwards. Holmes, the visitors' cus- todian, made some good' clearances, • whilst Boyd also ' kicked well, and • when the final whistle went the home, team had run out winners by six "goals to none.,

NORTH CHESHIRE LEAGUE.

Lateliford's Fine Win.

The Latchford Club, at BrOadheath, against Altrincham Lads, were minus the services of Grace, Webb and Fogg of the selected team, and substitutes were found in Stubbs, Siddall (brother to the Eccles Borough player) and Trainer Tinder, and with their playing strength weakened, and Challinor having missed- his train, they had to play a man short until he arrived,, and thus at the beginning their chances of victory were not very rosy. The players taking part were :—Latchford—Green ; Robinson and Stubbs; Siddall, Downs and Carter; Smith, Challinor, Lees, Taylor and Tinder. Altrincham Lads—Hall; W. Boss awl G. Smith; J. Owen, M. Walker and L. Walker; T. Owen, Brood, Kemp, Barnett and Bowla.d. Referee—J. Dewson. _

_ The game opened out evenly, both, sides attac.king in turn, and the respective backs made some fine returns, while the forwards combined very well. The home goalkeeper accomplished some fine work between the sticks. At half-time the visitors were leading by three goals to two. On resuming, the game was keenly fought. Green was at fault with bad play, allowing two easy shots to count against his side, whilst Lees, for the visitors, was very busy doing the scoring at the ether end, he being responible for all the goals scored roe his iide. Thiel& 'made i very 'conspicuous run Om the heft, finishing with a fine centre. Lees metithe.ball and sent it just over the bar. When the .whistle blew for time the visitors were *inners by five goals to four. The home' team-played well' to a Man. while Downs, Lees. C,hallinor,- Smith, and Tinder *ere' MO pick bt the visitors. . _

Crostield's Pile Up a Score.

The Crosfield's Recreation team met the Knutsford M.I. in the North Cheshire League, and the Crosfield's side- was :—Birehall ; Raw- linson and Tindall Clemo, Worrall and H. Sherratt ; Beddall, Whitfield, Hawthorn, Foster and T. Sherratt. Crosfield's won the toss and soon began to show that they were out to win. During the first half they did most qf the press- ing, and pig four goals to their ,account, Birchall, the visitors' goalkeeper, only having to handle the ball on three oceasionsjand once he made a ,glorious save ,when ,he appeared to be well beaten. Half-time arrived in favour of the visitors by four goals to nil. -

_ The second half was more in favour of the yisitors than the first moiety of the game had been, and the home team could not hold their opponents. • Six goals were added to the score, while the home team only replied on two occa- sions, when goals were obtained chiefly owing to slack play on the part of the visitors' backs, who allowed the home forwards to break through. When time was called Crosfleld's ran out easy winners by 10 goals to two. The visitors' goal scorers were Clemo, one from a nenalty kick; Beddall, one; Whitfield,. three; Hawthorn and Foster, two each; and T. Sherratt, one.

WARRINGTON AND DISTRICT LEAGUE.

The First Division.

, In, the, Warrington and District League the Great Sankey team were entertained by the Weston (Runcorn) team, and, owing to the reputation which the Visitors had attained last season, when they were members Of the Widnes Alliance, the villagers turned out in good num- bers to watehthe game, which the Great Sankey team won. by four goals to one. The Weaver Refining Company's team should have visited Orford to play the Barracks team, but the fixture was cancelled late in the Week, owing to the Weaver Club not being able to get a team together, owing to other kinds of sport taking place at Acton Bridget The Divex Club also cancelled their fixture with Croslield's "A " team, owing to most of their" players.being away On holiday. -

Second Division.

It( the, Second Division, Warrington Excel- sior were entertained by Fairfield on their ground at Padgate-lane! The game during the iirst half was evenly contested, the visitors leading by one goal to nil. In the second por- tion the visitors brought heavy pressure to bear on the home goal, and had it not been for Dainteth in goal the score would have been I much larger. He was badly supported by his backs, and the Excelsior* Lads were easy whiners by eight goals to none. Orford St. John's were at .home to the Stock- ton Heath "A" team, and, the latter, accom- panied, by a, good. number of their. supporters, were in high hopes of being able to bring about the first defeat of St. John's, but their hopes were not realised.. They vanished as a dream as the .game ,prOgressed, and at half- time St; John's were four goals up.. During the Second portion the "Heathens "- were defending their goal to the best of their ability, and Orford St. John's added two more goals to their score, and they had two goals disallowed for offside. Holmes (goalkeeper) and Boyd (full-back) were the only players of note for the visitors, ,while the home team played a good game all round, their goalkeeper only handling the ball on four. occasions. The match at Lovely-lane between Bank Quay Bees. and St. Barnabas' proved to be very keen and exciting. These two being neighbouring clubs,- and sharing' the right of playing on the same ground, drew a good crowd together to witness the'game, which was evenly contested. WARRINGTON BLUE COAT CHARITY SCHOOL. NOTIQE IS HEREBY GIVEN that the Trustees of the above School will, at their next Quarterly Meeting, on the 20th day of October next, proceed to elect Three Children (3 boys) to Vacancies now existing in the Institution. No Candidate will be eligible unless an application on his behalf (forma of which may be obtained from the Clerk, at his Office, 3, Winwick-street), shall have been made to the Clerk to the Trustees, at least fourteen days previous to the day of meeting. In electing Children, the Trusteea make Orphans* their first choice. Those deprived of one parent come next in consideration, and after them the children of parents who have the greatest number and the slenderest means. Dated this 19th day of September. 1913. HENRY GREENALL, Clerk to the Trustees.

WITH THE WOODS. President's Main Provides a Good "Wind-up " at the Liberal Club. J. SMITH'S SUCCESS. One only needs to visit some of the bowling greens in town to be convinced that the trundlers' season is almost over. There are practically only two competitions concluding now—the Works' Association Individual Merit Competition and that connected with the War- rington and District Association. There were great doings on the Liberal Club green on Saturday, when the President's Main —the last Contest of the season—was com- menced, the final being played on Monday even- ing, when J. Smith was the successful bowler. There were 70 entries for the 16 valuable prizes, and the main was highly successful. The bowl- ing club have had a great season, chiefly owing to the offices of Mr. Fred Walker, the energetic secretary. The games on Saturday were very interesting, and general satisfaction with the whole arrange- ments was expressed. There was a preliminary round and four rounds proper, with semi-final and final games. A great deal of enthusiasm was manifested, and ,each game was played in a thoroughly sportsmanlike manner. In the semi-final J. Benn ion beat J. Davies and J. Smith beat C. H. Hibbert. The final game was intensely exciting. Both .players had a start of. one point, and all througli it was a pretty even game. Smith led' off with a couple and a single, but at the 10th end Ben- nion peeled with six. Another double and a

This, week a start has been made with the work of demolish- ing the south-east corner Of Market Gate, with a view to con- tinuing the plan of Making Market Gale into a Circus. 'Cor- poration workmen are busy at the rear of the. property of the Buttermarket and Bridge-street shops, and a breach hits ben Made to the front of- Bridge-street on-the premises formerly otictipied by Messrs. Boot: There have been- some finds of interest, notably'a well to the rear Of the old Fox Inn, supposed 'to be about 300 years old. • SOME DISCOVERIES. The old well was covered over by a flag and three feet of earth, but below this was found a yawning chasm. Into this our representative descended by means of a rope ladder, steadied by workmen engaged on the job. The dimensions of the well were found to be: Depth, 361 t.; width at top, 31t., widening considerably below where•the 'flag had been. A large cellar over .10ft. across and nearly 7ft..high, which had prob- ably been part of the Fox Inn, was exposed to view. The ceil- ing was very wet and provided an uncomfortable drip upon the head of the explorer while he descended yet another stage towards the water-level, at which the well was from 3ft. 4in. to 41 t. in diameter. There was soft clay at the bottom. In the centre was the iron bore; three inches wide, and inside was -three feet of water. The air in the well was pure. At intervals wooden props, in splendid condition, supported the sides. The whole structure bore very few signs of decay. , A few yards from the well is another hole, which is believed to commugicate with an underground archway. The well will be filled up with about 50 tons of rubbish. SIGNS OF GREAT ANTIQUITY. Alderman Bennett Made the descent of the well, and says it bears signs of great antiquity, B,nd is probably 300 to 400 years old; that many old wells have been discovered in the course of the demolition of property in the town, but, speaking generally, this is one of the largest, deepest and most interesting. An old. pump found over the Well was taken to the Howley Yard belonging to the Corporation. TIIE DISAPPEARING "FOX." The Fox Inn itself is one of the most interesting ancient buildings in the town. How ancient is the Fox Inn? No one seems to know anything..as to its'origin or its age. What is to become of it now? It was occupied lately by and for some considerable time it was thought the late Mr. Hobert Lewis, as the Old Curiosity that that was the year in whiCh the inn was Shop,-but -a,t'present it is untenanted. built. •Alderman Bennett informed us that in all On the doorpost in Buttermarket-street is en- probability the bilikling will be removed some- graved the appended words, and hero the date where and rebuilt, but that nothing definite had 1704 appears. , been decided.
